金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 9 月 30 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 美國企業投資動能維持韌性 ：8 月標普全球美國綜合 PMI 升至 58.4，創2021 年 7 月來高點 ，
製造業、服務業同步擴張至 57 和 58.7； 8 月扣除飛機與國防的核心資本財訂單月增 1.6%，
高於前月修正後的 0.6%。其中，初級金屬、機械及電機設備訂單均呈現成長，顯示企業資本
支出、AI 投資與國防需求仍具韌性，持續支撐美國經濟成長。
◼ 美中同意貿易休兵再延長兩個月：美中同意調降雙邊總值 600 億美元商品的關稅，涵蓋美國

玉米、化妝品及中國家電、玩具等產品，並延長貿易休兵至明年 1 月 10 日，同時中國承諾繼
續供應稀土並擴大採購美國農產品與煤礦，美國則繼續暫停實施 50%穿透性規則；AI 方面，
美中雙方雖未就 AI 監管議題達成共識 ， 但同意建立風險溝通機制 ， 並於11 月底前展開對話 。
◼ Meta 旗下 AI 代理人 Muse 登頂排行榜：Meta 新款個人 AI 代理 Muse 上線後迅速累積逾
250 萬次下載，並登上美國 App Store 下載排行榜首位。相較過去生成式 AI 以問答與內容生
成為主，Muse 可進一步協助安排日程、搜尋資訊、處理文件及完成交易，代表 AI 正從提升
效率工具逐步走向數位勞動力，市場評價焦點也將逐漸由 AI 基礎設施擴散至應用軟體業者。
市場展望
◼ AI 需求持續擴張，科技供應鏈成長動能延續：AI 基礎建設需求持續擴張，帶動 HBM、伺服器
DRAM、 先進製程與封裝等關鍵供應鏈維持成長動能 ， 市場表現由全面上漲逐步轉向結構性分
化。若 AI 伺服器需求持續成長且 CSP 資本支出維持高檔，具供給優勢、定價能力及較高訂單

能見度之企業，其營運表現有機會獲得支撐。整體而言，科技產業獲利表現仍具關注度，惟後
續仍須留意全球景氣、產業供需變化、政策監管及市場評價調整等風險因素。
◼ 債券佈局可關注中天期美國公債及投等債：中天期美國公債及投資級債仍值得持續關注：**雖
然貨幣政策風險下降有助於穩定長期通膨預期 ， 但政府與企業發債供給增加所帶來的排擠效應 ，
以及美國經濟成長展現韌性，仍可能使長天期利率面臨上行壓力。因此，在當前利率環境下，
中天期債券於票息收益與存續期間風險之間相對較為均衡。投資級債方面，彭博美國投資級公
司債指數利差約 80 個基點，雖然利差偏窄，但高基準利率環境仍提供相對較高的收益水準。
因此，在目前市場環境下，中天期美國公債及投資級債仍為值得持續觀察的債券資產類別，惟
後續表現仍須留意利率走勢、信用利差變化及經濟成長動能等因素。

Mediatek (2454.TW)

Earnings upward revision on better OPM, robust
AI ASIC revenue on track; maintain Buy with TP
at NT$6,800
Mediatek will host an analyst meeting on Oct 30; we expect an inline GM/OPM
(guidance mid-point of 46%/15%), yet we expect a strong c30% QoQ growth
outlook for 4Q26 thanks to AI ASIC contribution and better mix/pricing for
consumer products. On the back of solid operating leverage, we are also looking for

OPM upside. We are looking for upbeat 4Q26 guidance.
With AI ASIC starts contributing in 4Q26 and multiplying growth into next two
years, we expect OPM expansion for Mediatek on the back of its operational
leverage. We lift our earnings projection for 2026/2027 /28 by 9%/ 25%/9%. We
maintain our Buy with TP$6,800 (30x of 2027 /28 EPS, from 35x previously, mainly
on recent macro concerns and concerns on AI capex beyond 2028).

### Page 7

Mediatek (2454.TW)
21 October 2026 Citi Research
7
Mediatek
Company description
Mediatek (MTK) is a fabless IC company that designs SoC for smartphone,
smart TV, and digital media devices. It also develops chips for connectivity,
networking, smart home gadgets, and automotive sectors. It was founded in
1997, is listed on the Taiwan Stock Exchange, and is headquartered in Taiwan.
It has sales and research subsidiaries in mainland China, Singapore, India,

the US, Japan, Korea, Denmark, and England. It primarily sells its products in
China and other emerging-market countries.
Investment strategy
We rate Mediatek shares as Buy. We expect Mediatek to ride on 5G migration
in emerging markets and market share gain of its high-end Dimensity series in
China market as well. In particular, we see strong growth potential from its AI
ASIC, NTN-satellite business in the long term. We believe solid fundamentals

and strong operating leverage will support Mediatek’s margins outlook.
Valuation
Our NT$6,800 target price (rounded) for Mediatek is based on 30x P/E to
2027 /2028 EPS, which is 1-std above its five-year average forward P/E given
the promising outlook from its AI ASIC business starting from 2H26. We
believe our target PE is also justified by Mediatek's global and regional peers'
trading at an average of c32x PER. AI ASIC development with CSPs should

provide more upside from 2027 onwards. We also see Mediatek as well
prepared for 6G and 6G+NTN Satellite communication. Despite lukewarm
smartphone overall deamand, we expect Mediatek to benefit from 5G
migration in emerging markets and its Dimensity series should also gain
shares in flagship models. Non-smartphone business is also seeing
stabilizing TV and Io T demand with Mediatek outgrowing the overall market
on its better technology in WiFi 7 and connectivity.

Risks
Key downside risks that could prevent the shares from reaching our target
price include: 1) weaker-than-expected growth from AI ASIC business; 2)
more severe competition in the smartphone segment; 3) slower overall
demand related to macroeconomic risks, especially for smartphone demand;
and 4) further price hikes from foundries.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.
Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst

expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment
rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient

valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating
and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of

information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price
chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and

investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the
expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.

Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:
Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in

reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is
moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.
Privately held companies:
The legal entities through which Citi conducts its business do not permit investment research recommendations, ratings,

not guaranteed. Private companies are generally not subject to reporting obligations, which may result in less publicly available
information. The information contained in this report may differ from information a private company may subsequently make
public or otherwise share with investors. Continuation of publishing private company reports or private company sections
within reports is at Citi's discretion, in accordance with internal policies.

The legal entities employing the authors of this report are listed below (and their regulators are listed further herein). Non-US
research analysts who have prepared this report (i.e., all research analysts listed below other than those identified as employed
by Citigroup Global Markets Inc.) are not registered/qualified as research analysts with FINRA. Such research analysts may not
be associated persons of the member organization (but are employed by an affiliate of the member organization) and therefore

may not be subject to the FINRA Rule 2241 restrictions on communications with a subject company, public appearances and

### Page 1

Powertech Technology

Up-cycle level of earnings to continue; 
reiterate Buy

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 500.00 TWD | Price: 254.50 TWD

Reiterate Buy on above-trend growth and margins 
We believe Powertech Technology Inc. (PTI) can continue to benefit from the AI boom 
and an upturn in the memory chip industry. 3Q results clearly confirm record-high 
revenue (NT$25bn; up 27% YoY) and an above-trend gross margin (24%; normal high 
teen% or 20% range). 4Q guidance (for packaging/testing businesses) also remained 
bullish: DRAM – tight supply vs strong demand; NAND – order increase for more value-
added NAND and enterprise SSD; and logic – mix improvement with FC-BGA and price 
hike (following cost increase). Key customers are Micron, Kioxia, Solidigm (owned by SK 
Hynix) and various Greater China logic chip makers that require outsourced packaging 
and testing services. As we have already assumed up-cycle earnings level for 2027-28, 
our EPS estimates remain largely unchanged. We maintain our PO of NT$500 (27x 2027-
28E P/E applied). Reiterate Buy.  
Key takeaways from 3Q results  
Five takeaways from 3Q results: (1) strong execution in newly growing packaging orders 
for high-end memory (low-power / high-speed DRAM, enterprise SSD, 200+ layers 
NAND), (2) increasing exposure to more advanced logic (through FC-BGA, etc), (3) a well-
balanced sales mix (DRAM: 20% of total sales; NAND: 28%; SiP/modules 9%; logic 43%), 
(4) notably improved gross margins (limited increase in manufacturing costs vs strong 
top-line growth seen), and (5) solid FCF generation (EBITDA: c.NT$8bn vs capex: 
NT$5bn; expected annual dividend yield: 3-4% range).

Key Metrics      
* For full definitions of iQmethod SM measures, see page 15.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
Headquartered in Taiwan, PTI is one of the largest OSAT 
firms, which packages and tests memory and logic chips. It 
has around 18,000 employees. The logic chip-related 
business operates under Greatek Electronics (fully 
consolidated into earnings). Management's new focus areas 
in packaging/testing are PLP, TSV, 3D NAND, low-power 
DRAM, SSD, etc.

Investment Rationale 
Our Buy rating is based on strong earnings growth, driven 
by more value-added memory and even logic packaging 
solutions. 1Q-3Q26 earnings have already hit upcycle levels, 
posting a strong YoY growth (largely driven by NAND/SSD). 
We believe the memory super-cycle and customers' 
ongoing outsourcing trend (Micron, Solidigm/Hynix, Kioxia 
and Greater China's logic chipmakers) using Powertech's 
proven backend solutions should be the key contributors to 
strong earnings growth by 2028.

Stock Data  
Price to Book Value 3.0x

Exhibit 1: Powertech is accelerating its advanced FOPLP packaging strategy through significant capacity expansion, strategic partnerships (including 
Broadcom), and customer wins such as AMD, positioning itself as a key beneficiary of growing AI semiconductor packaging demand from 2027 onward 
Powertech’s recent news summary during Jul-Aug 2026 
Date Key news Summary 
Aug-2026 FOPLP 
production 
-Powertech plans to start production at the world's first panel-level chip packaging facility for AI chips as soon as early 2027 to make server CPUs, AI 
computing chips and advanced optics 
-Powertech said its advanced FOPLP production line has secured a 100% UTR through 2030 ahead of its scheduled volume productio n in 2027 
-Powertech plans to invest NT$70bn (US$2.2bn) in its FOPLP facility, with installed capacity set to increase from 1,000 panels per months in 2026 
to 5,000 in 2028 
Jul-2026 JV with Broadcom -Powertech reported plans to invest US$400mn to create a new JV with Broadcom in Singapore to cater to customer demand for advanced panel-
based packaging services 
-The JV will concentrate on offering additive finer-geometry redistribution layer (RDL) tech for advanced package substrates  
 AMD business -Powertech reaffirmed that it is on track to start shipping AI chips utilizing its advanced PLP tech for AMD in 2H27, dismissing concern s over delays 
-AMD will be one of Powertech's first customers adopting the FOPLP tech, which is considered to be more cost-effective than the mainstream 
CoWoS tech 
  Capex/capacity -Powertech plans to acquire the entire Philippine unit of ON Semiconductor for US$45mn as part of its efforts to navigate global supply chain 
realignment amid escalating geopolitical risks 
-The transaction will be conducted by its subsidiary, Greatek Electronics in which Powertech holds 43% stake

### Page 13

Price objective basis & risk 
Powertech Technology (PWOGF) 
Our PO of NT$500 is derived from 27x P/E based on 2027-28E EPS. This is 10-20% 
higher than 2024-25 peak levels (low- to mid-20x). Since we expect 50%+ higher EPS in 
2026-28E vs 2024-25 on the back of record-high sales/margin, we believe fair multiples 
should be at least on par with the previous peak level. True, the long-term historical 
average P/E came in the range of 10-20x, but the memory super-cycle and AI boom 
should warrant above-trend earnings growth and consequently over 20x fair P/E. 
Taiwan's OSAT stocks also trade at 20-30x P/E.

Upside risk is mostly related to strong packaging orders from memory chipmakers and 
even logic chip suppliers.

Downside risk is mostly associated with an unexpected memory industry downturn or AI 
bubble (e.g., significant packaging order cut).

### Page 1

3Q recap - strong op leverage offset GPM pressure — 3Q26 net profits (+125% y/y) 
topped our/BBG estimates 35%/28% respectively owing mostly to stronger sales 
and larger degree of op leverage. Sales reached a record NT$32.4bn (+24% q/q and 
+83% y/y), 23%/12% above Citi/BBG estimate, which can be attributable to strong 
demand in edge AI, semi equipment and smart healthcare applications. GPM fell to 
36.7% (-1ppt q/q and -2.2ppt y/y) below Citi/BBG estimates of 38.0%/37.6%, 
reflecting higher component prices. Nevertheless, OPM expanded to 20.9%, 
underpinned by strong op leverage on higher sales scale. OP profits +151% y/y beat 
Citi/BBG forecast 33%/21%, the key driver to the earnings beat.    
Implications — Strong order intake is encouraging with 3Q BB ratio reaching 1.6x 
and NA particularly robust at 2.1x. However, we believe the stock's rich valuation 
limits further re-rating potential, particularly as a higher comparison base could 
moderate growth in 2027E. Continued GPM compression also reinforces our 
concerns over material/component inflation and the timing of price increases to 
offset higher costs. Advantech will host 3Q26 results call on Nov 4th. We will revisit 
our numbers and share key takeaways following management's updates.

Valuation
Our target price of NT$670 is based on 35x 2H26-1H27E EPS, at the high-cycle of the stock's valuation range between 20-35x
since 2020, reflecting solid BB ratio, improving margin profile and ROE.
Risks
Key upside risks that could cause Advantech’s shares to trade above our target price include: 1) better-than-expected
marcoeconomic outlook; and 2) better-than-expected demand and margin profile. Key downside risks would include: 1)

slower-than-expected demand recovery, especially the China market; and 2) further deterioration of demand in US and EU
markets.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 10 月 28 日

1 群益投顧 
研究員：吳建弘 A51102@cim.capital.com.tw

08/17/2026 Buy 200.00

公司基本資訊  
目前股本 (百萬元 ) 3,746

現金股息配發率 (%) 91.58

產品組合

股價相對大盤走勢

長榮航太 (2645 TT)  Trading Buy

預期波音 737 系列交機速度將略為放緩。  
投資建議 ：雖然 2026 年長榮航太的維修業務持續擴張，但考量
波音將維持 737 系列月均交機一段時間，可能會影響長榮航太
的製造部門復甦情形，故給予長榮航太 Trading Buy 的投資建
議。  
波音 737 系列月交機將維持一段時間，調整長榮航太後續製造
的營運預估： 波音於 10/27/2026 公告 3Q26 財報，並於法說會
上提及 :「當前需維持 737 系列月均交機量穩定在 47 架一段時
間，後續管理階層預期交機提升可能要到 2H27 才會上升至 52
架」，使得長榮航太的製造部門復甦仍需等待一段時間，故研
究部一併調整長榮航太後續的營運預估。  
3Q26 先前的維修漲價陸續貢獻，營運維持高檔： 3Q26 長榮航
太自 2H24 起陸續針對維修業務進行漲價後，加上全球機隊持
續供不應求，增加維修機率與維修產值，惟製造部門雖然波音
已將 737 系列交機上升至 47 架 /月，但短期拉貨力道仍有限，
使得長榮航太的製造業務與轉投資公司營運維持低檔，營運仍
以維修業務為主， 3Q26 營收 52.75 億元， QoQ+1.02% ，預估稅
後純益 8.31 億元， QoQ+10.34% ， EPS 2.22 元。  
2027 年空中巴士首件交付、無人機新訂單，有望成為中長期營
運新動能： 2027 年長榮航太除了持續受惠維修業務與波音交機
的逐年擴張之外，在新訂單的部分，先前與空中巴士的新訂單，
預估 1Q27 將迎來首件交付，並待後續取得特殊製程認證後，
2H27 有望迎來新訂單的量產，以及無人機訂單也有望成為長榮
航太新一波的營運動能，預估 2027 年營收 224.86 億元，
YoY+7.85% ，稅後純益 37.61 億元， YoY+17.06% ，EPS 10.04 元。

3 群益投顧 
圖一、全球商用飛機平均機齡

資料來源 : IATA ，長榮航太  
波音 737 系列月交機將維持一段時間，調整長榮航太後續製造的營運預估：
波音於 10/27/2026 公告 3Q26 財報，並於法說會上提及 :「當前需維持 737 系
列月均交機量穩定在 47 架一段時間，後續管理階層預期交機提升可能要到
2H27 才會上升至 52 架」，使得長榮航太的製造部門復甦仍需等待一段時
間，故研究部一併調整長榮航太後續的營運預估。  
2027 年空中巴士首件交付、無人機新訂單，有望成為中長期營運新動能：  
2027 年長榮航太除了持續受惠維修業務與波音交機的逐年擴張之外，在新
訂單的部分，先前與空中巴士的新訂單，預估 1Q27 將迎來首件交付，並待
後續取得特殊製程認證後， 2H27 有望迎來新訂單的量產，以及無人機訂單
也有望成為長榮航太新一波的營運動能，預估 2027 年營收 224.86 億元，
YoY+7.85% ，稅後純益 37.61 億元， YoY+17.06% ， EPS 10.04 元。  
投資建議：  
雖然 2026 年長榮航太的維修業務持續擴張，但考量波音將維持 737 系列月
均交機一段時間，可能會影響長榮航太的製造部門復甦情形，故給予長榮航
太 Trading Buy 的投資建議。

7 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

本刊載之報告為台新投顧於特定日期之分析，已力求陳述內容之可靠性，純屬研究性質，僅作參考，使用者應明瞭內容之時效性，
審慎考量投資風險，並就投資結果自行負責。報告著作權屬台新投顧所有，禁止任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。 
台新證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市仁愛路四段 118 號 16 樓(02)5589-9558  (113) 金管投顧新字第 021 號 
公司拜訪快報

曜越(3540)：2027 年 Gaming PC 與 AI 工 
建議：評等買進

一、曜越為電競電腦零組件大廠，積極切入AI 高效能工
作站系統 
曜越科技股份有限公司成立於 1999 年 11 月，為電腦機
殼、散熱器(模組)及電源供應器製造商，以自有品牌
Thermaltake 銷售桌上型電腦散熱模組、機殼、電源供
應器，專攻 clone 遊戲玩家市場，近年來公司積極整合
機殼、散熱、電源與系統配置能力，從單一產品銷售延
伸至完整 Gaming PC 系統解決方案，滿足高階 
Gaming PC 的效能與穩定性需求。4Q26 公司將切入
AI 工作站(AI Workstation)新業務 ，拓展地端 AI 運算
市場。2026 年 1~9 月主要產品營收占比，Gaming PC 
35%、電源供應器 33%、機殼 14%、散熱 13%、其他
5%。

二、2Q26 本業疲弱，EPS 0.01 元 
曜越 2Q26 營收 10.16 億元，年減 20.5%，毛利率則從
前期 28.1%升至 30.7%，主要是因記憶體漲價，使毛利
率較佳的客製化裝機業務佔比提升， 2Q26 稅後淨利
96.8 萬元，EPS 0.01 元。 
三、曜越從 PC 零組件品牌轉型為系統整合品牌，並積
極切入 AI 工作站市場  
展望下半年 ，3Q26 營收年減 2.9%， 估計3Q26 稅後 EPS 
0.09 元。4Q 通常為傳統旺季，加上 AI 工作站從雲端到
地端的基本條件已於 2026 年第三季成熟，產品規劃完
成，第四季開始進入市場，出貨量將逐步上升。 
2026 年前九月 Gaming PC 營收佔比已達 35%， 超越電
源供應器的 33%，成為最大產品線，象徵系統整合業務
已成為營收核心。曜越的電競 PC 分為三個價格層次：
入門級 1,599-1,799 美元、進階級 2,599 美元、高階
4,399-4,499 美元。其中核心零組件如機殼、散熱、電源
皆是自製，而主機板、CPU、GPU、SSD 等為外購，透
過美國、德國、澳洲、中國等海外子公司進行在地組裝
與銷售。展望下一階段，曜越將複製電競 PC 的成功模
式，推出五款 AI 工作站與 AI PC 產品線，從入門級 AI
電競 PC（View 380 XL，1,801 TOPS）到企業旗艦雙
系統 AI 工作站（A1500 Dual，16,000 TOPS） ，搭載
NVIDIA RTX 5090、RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell 及 AMD 
Radeon AI PRO R9700 等高階 GPU。AI 工作站從雲端
到地端的基本條件已於 2026 年第三季成熟，產品規劃
完成，第四季開始進入市場，2027 年營收貢獻將逐季顯
現 ， 目標2027 年占整體業績一成 ， 主要鎖定AI 開發者、
專業創作者、學術科研及中小企業等應用。這代表曜越
正式從傳統電競跨足企業級 AI 運算、專業開發與數位
創作的地端商機，由於商用工作站毛利通常優於一般消
費級產品，這將是明年拉升獲利結構與擴展新市場的最
大看點。 
四、評等買進理由 
投顧認為曜越已從機箱、電源供應器及散熱等 PC 零組
件，轉型至拓展更高毛利率的電競PC 系統整合與 AI 工
作站業務，使 2026 年整體毛利率有望升到 30%以上，
預期 2027 年營收將成長 15%，估計 2026 年 EPS 0.46
元，2027 年 EPS 1.95 元，給予買進的建議，目標價31
元。(16x2027EPS)。

(26.10.29)

자동차업종

◼ 3Q26 비용절감/환율 효과로 우려 대비 양호한 실적 시현 
◼ 2027년 물량–환율 영향 상쇄되며 2H27 이익체력 개선 본격화 예상 
◼ 1) Physical AI(로봇/SDV) 경쟁력, 2) 주요국 점유율 반등에 주목

투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 50만원 하향(-6%) 
현대차 TP 50만원(시총 102조원)은 12MF EPS 41,978원에 Target PER 12배
적용하여 산출. 제한적인 실적 기대 반영해 타겟 밸류 하향함에 따라 TP 조정  
우려 대비 양호한 3Q26 실적으로 주가 하방 부담 완화. 주가의 추세적 상승 
위해서는 1) Physical AI(로봇/SDV) 경쟁력, 2) 주요국 점유율 반등 확인돼야 할 
것. 기저효과에 따른 물량 개선이 주가 저점 받쳐줄 것이나, 현재 밸류(2027년 
PER 9.1배vs 글로벌 피어 6.1배)에서 추세 상승 이끌긴 어려울 것이기 때문. 
이에, 자동차 본업 외 1) 한/미국 Physical AI 투자 계획 및 2) BD 공급망 구축 
/양산 가시화에 따른 주가 반등 기대

3Q26 매출 45조(YoY -3%, 이하 YoY), OP 2.7조(+6%), OPM 5.9% 시현 
3Q26 OP 추정(2.5조)/컨센(2.4조) 상회. 파업에 따른 생산/판매 차질 영향으로 
기대 제한적이었으나, 비용 절감/환율 영향으로 우려 대비 양호한 실적 시현. 
[자동차] 매출 33조(-10%), OP 2.1조(+25%), OPM 6.5%. 도매 판매 92.1만대
(-11%). 판매 부진과 원자재 부담에도 불구, 환효과(+1.3조)/비용절감(+0.5조)
로 실적 방어. 이외 YoY 변수별 영향은 물량(-1.1조)/믹스(-0.6조)/원자재(-
0.5조). [금융] 매출 9.2조(+28%), OP 4,710억(-18%), OPM 5.1%, [기타] 매출 
2.9조(+2%), OP 712억(-72%), OPM 2.5% 기록

2027년 매출 191조(YoY +2%, 이하 YoY), OP 12.7조(+15%), OPM 6.6% 전망 
기저효과 바탕으로 연결 도매 물량 YoY +5% 증가 예상하나, 원달러 환율 -
4% 하락(가정 환율 1,380원)하며 1H27 → 2H27로가며 이익체력 개선 예상

### Page 5

DAISHIN SECURITIES

5  
  현대차 3Q26 실적 컨퍼런스콜 QnA 
Q. 금융부문 환율 변동에 따른 4Q26 매출 및 손익 영향 
- 금융부문 환율 영향은 현대차 실적에 미치는 환율 영향과 동일한 방향으로 발생

Q. 3Q26 금융부문 수익성 악화 요인 
- 장기금리 상승에 따른 조달 및 이자비용 증가가 주요인. 자산/부채 만기 매칭에 따른 
금리 상승 영향 반영 
- EV 중심 중고차 가격지수 소폭 하락 및 차량 반환에 따른 매각손실, 금리 상승에 따
른 연체율 및 신용비용 상승 
- 차량 판매가격 인하 영향은 없으며, 3Q26 도매 판매 감소에 따른 금융 영업수익 영향 
불가피했음

Q. 향후 주주환원 정책에서 배당 및 자사주 매입 우선순위 
- 기존 밸류업 정책: 최소 DPS 1만원/TPR 35% 제시. 연간 배당 약 2.6조원 → TPR 
35% 충족 위한 잔여분 자사주 매입/소각 활용 
- 경영환경 변화 고려하여 기존 밸류업 프로그램 전반의 주주가치 제고 방안 재검토중 
- 새로운 정책 확정 및 주주 소통 전까지 기존 약속인 최소 DPS 1만원 유지할 계획 
- 시장의 배당 및 자사주 매입 간 균형 요구를 인지하고 있으며, 주주 의견 수렴 후 주
주가치 제고 계획 공유 예정

Q. 3Q26 영업이익 증감 요인 중 기타 +6,400억 발생 원인 
- 월별 컨틴전시 활동을 통한 비용 최적화 및 재료비 절감 지속 추진 
- 4Q26 원가 절감 효과는 3Q26 대비 확대 예상

Q. 3Q26 영업외손익 감소 원인 
- 3Q26 환율 급락으로 외화 매출채권 등 외화자산 관련 외화환산손실 및 외환거래손실 
발생 
- 매출채권 인식 시점과 회수 시점 간 환율 차이에 따른 외환차손 발생 
- 지분법 대상 기업들의 3Q26 실적 부진에 따른 지분법손익 감소도 일부 영향. 대부분 
외환 관련 손실에서 발생

Q. 중국 OEM 국내 진출 확대에 따른 대응 및 신차/가격 전략 
- 중국 OEM 및 테슬라의 국내 M/S 확대 대응중. 다만, 잦은 가격 조정은 중고차 잔존
가치 및 고객가치 훼손할 수 있어 지양 
- 현대캐피탈의 캡티브 금융 경쟁력 활용. 잔존가치 유예할부 등을 통해 월 납입금을 낮
춰 중국 OEM 대비 경쟁력 확보 
- 서비스 네트워크 기반으로 경쟁사 대비 경쟁력 확보 노력 
- 국내 신차: 4Q26 아반떼 HEV, 투싼, GV90, G80 HEV, 싼타페 FL(2026.12월), G90 
FL 등 출시 예정 
- 북미: 4Q26 투싼, GV80 HEV 출시 및 27년 초 GV90 출시 예상 
- 유럽: 아이오닉3 판매 개시, 4Q26 투싼/베이온 등 주요 모델 출시 예정 
- 중국: 아이오닉 V / 인도: 베이온, 아이오닉 P 등 출시 예정 
- 신차 출시 계획 연도별로 다수 예정. 가격은 원가/경쟁사/소비자 가격 수용성 등을 고
려해 결정

### Page 6

현대차(005380)

Q. 3Q26 법인세비용 증가 원인 및 4Q26 전망 
- 일회성 요인보다는 법인별 손익 믹스 및 적용 세율 차이에 따른 영향 
- 4Q26 법인세 수준은 QoQ 큰 변동 없을 것으로 전망

## Document 245: AI関連投資の失速は日本にどう波及するか ～日銀の金融システムレポートから考察する三つの経路～

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI関連投資の失速は日本にどう波及するか ～日銀の金融システムレポートから考察する三つの経路～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Research Report

2026年10月 29日

1/8 
AI関連投資の失速は日本にどう波及するか 
～日銀の金融システムレポートから考察する三つの経路～ 
 資産運用調査部 主席研究員  奥田 宏二

（要旨） 
○ 日銀は 2026年 10月の金融システムレポートで、人工知能（AI）関連投資への期待が縮小し
た場合の金融安定上のリスクを分析した。本稿では同レポートに基づき日本へのリスクの波
及経路を整理したうえで、金融機関のストレス耐性を確認する。 
○ 米国の巨大IT企業によるAI関連投資が急拡大している。これらの企業は従来の自己資金に
加え、社債発行などによる外部資金調達を増やしている。データセンター事業では、長期リ
ース契約を裏付けとした銀行融資や、プライベートファンドによる投融資が広がっている。
AI関連の旺盛な資金需要は経済成長につながる一方、特定分野へのリスクの集中と表裏一体
の関係にある。 
○ 日本への影響の波及経路として、銀行融資、金融市場、実体経済の三つが考えられる。日本
の大手行は海外のデータセンター事業者やファンドへの融資を増やしている。また、AI関連
株の下落をきっかけにヘッジファンドが投資ポジションを縮小すれば、日本の株式市場だけ
でなく、国債市場にも影響が及ぶ可能性がある。AI関連投資の減速によるアジア経済の悪化
も、日本の金融機関の信用コスト増加につながり得る。 
○ 日銀のストレステストでは、AI関連投資への期待の縮小とノンバンクによるショックの増幅
を想定しても、日本の金融機関は相応の耐性を備えているとの結果となった。一方、AI関連
企業の間では出資と将来の売上高の約束が結びついた循環的な取引がみられ、その影響はハ
イパースケーラーを通じて日本の大手行のデータセンター向け融資にも間接的に及び得る。
取引の連鎖全体を見る視点が必要だ。

1. はじめに 
日銀は10月23日、金融システムレポートを公表した。IMFが先に公表した国際金融安定性報告
書（GFSR）と同様、金融安定上のリスク要因として人工知能（AI）に焦点を当てた。AI関連企業
の成長期待を背景に、世界の株式市場では株価上昇が続き、データセンターなどへの設備投資も
拡大している。一方、投資規模が膨らむにつれて、巨額の投資に見合った収益が実現するかどう
かが金融市場の大きな関心事となっている。

### Page 2

2/8 
今回、注目したいのはAI関連投資と海外のノンバンク金融仲介機関（NBFI）の動向だ。AI関連
投資への期待が縮小した場合、海外ノンバンクの資産売却などを通じてショックが増幅され、日
本の金融市場にも波及する可能性を日銀は指摘している。本稿では、日銀の分析をもとに、AI関
連投資を支える資金調達の変化と、海外ノンバンクを通じた日本へのリスクの波及経路を整理し
たうえで、金融機関のストレス耐性を確認する。

2. 拡大するAI投資とノンバンクの役割 
AI関連投資は近年、大幅に増えている。日銀の図表によると、米国の巨大IT企業（ハイパース
ケーラー）5社の設備投資額は、2022年頃の四半期約400億ドルから2026年 4～6月期には約
1,800億ドルと4倍以上に増加した（注1） 。これらの企業は従来、潤沢な営業キャッシュフロー
をもとにデータセンター投資を行ってきた。ところが、足元では社債発行などによる外部資金調
達を増やすようになった。投資額の大きいデータセンターを例に見ていこう。 
ハイパースケーラーでは、自社でデータセンターを建設するだけでなく、外部事業者と長期リ
ース契約を結んで利用権を確保する動きも目立っている。5社の未開始のリース契約額は2026年
4～6月期に約1.1兆ドルに達した（資料1、注1）。

資料 1 米ハイパースケーラー5社の設備投資と資金調達

（注）集計対象はAlphabet、Amazon、Meta、Microsoft、Oracleの 5社。①の「ネット借入・社債発行」と「ネット株式発行」は、プラス
が資金調達。直近は 2026年 4～6月期。数値は日本銀行の図表から筆者が読み取った概数。 
（出所）日本銀行「金融システムレポート」（2026年 10月号）図表Ⅴ-2-8（原資料はLSEG Workspace、SEC）をもとに第一ライフ資
産運用経済研究所作成

データセンターを建設・保有する事業者は、ハイパースケーラーとの長期リース契約を裏付け
に、銀行などから建設資金を調達している。銀行が融資を組成し、その貸出債権をプライベート
ファンドが購入する事例や、ファンドがデータセンター事業に直接出資する事例もある。こうし
て、AI関連投資を支える資金の出し手として、銀行に加えてプライベートファンドなどのNBFIの

### Page 3

3/8 
存在感が高まっている。 
日銀が集計した2023年1月から 2026年8月までに公表された米国のデータセンター案件で
は、資金調達額は合計約1.6兆ドルに上る。このうち調達済みは約0.2兆ドルにとどまり、約1.4
兆ドルが計画段階にある。また、案件数の約2割でプライベートファンドがスポンサーとなって
いる（注2）。 
さらに、AI関連企業の間では、出資と将来の売上高が結びついた循環的な取引もみられる。IMF
が2026年10月に公表したGFSRによると、大手IT企業などが財務基盤の相対的に弱いAI企業に出
資し、出資を受けた企業が出資元などにサービスや半導体を発注する事例がある。IMFが確認した
2023年以降の14案件では、約3,000億ドルの出資と約6,500億ドルの債務調達が、約1.4兆ドル
の将来の売上高の約束と結びついている。出資先の経営が悪化すれば、将来の売上高と投融資の
価値が同時に損なわれる可能性がある。 
こうした資金調達の拡大によって、AI関連投資と銀行、プライベートファンドなどの結びつき
が強まっている。AI関連投資が期待どおりの収益を生まなければ、データセンター事業者への融
資や出資を通じて、銀行やファンドに損失が生じる可能性がある。さらに、銀行はファンド自体
にも融資しており、ファンドの財務状況の悪化が銀行に波及する経路もある。日銀は、こうした
金融主体間の結びつきを通じて、ショックが金融システムに広がる可能性を警戒している。

3. 日本に及ぶ三つの波及経路 
AI関連投資の収益性に対する懸念の高まりや投資そのものの減速に直面した場合、日本の金融
システムにはどのような影響が及ぶのか。日銀の分析をもとに、①銀行融資、②金融市場、③実
体経済――の三つの波及経路からみていく。

（1） 銀行の融資を通じた経路 
日本の大手行は海外でデータセンター事業者やプライベートファンドへの融資を増やしてい
る。日銀によると、2026年 3月末時点で3メガ行の海外貸出全体に占めるデータセンター向けの
割合は 3％、海外ファンド向けは9％だった。また、3メガ行の海外ファンド向け貸出残高は12兆
円と、前年同月末から29.2％増加した。海外貸出全体に占める割合は現時点で限定的だが、収益
性の高さなどから注力分野となっている。データセンター事業者には賃料収入を特定の巨大IT企
業に依存するケースがあり、AI投資が期待どおりの収益を生まなければ、融資の返済能力にも影
響しかねない。また、技術革新によって高性能半導体などの設備が短期間で陳腐化するリスクも
ある。 
海外ファンド向け融資も注視すべきだろう。銀行はファンドに直接融資しているほか、ファン
ドが投資する企業や事業にも資金を供給している。ファンド向け貸出は、信用力の高い投資家の
出資約束を担保とする形式が多く、投資適格の比率は高い。ただ、日銀は、海外ファンドの運用
実態や資産価値を適時に把握するのは必ずしも容易ではないと指摘する。市場で大きなショック
が生じれば、ファンドが契約済みの融資枠から資金を引き出し、銀行の資金供給負担が増える可

### Page 4

4/8 
能性もある。

（2） 金融市場を通じた経路 
日本株の上昇は、AI関連需要の恩恵が相対的に大きい電機、精密、非鉄、機械などの業種がけ
ん引してきた。日銀は、AI関連投資に見合った収益拡大が実現しない場合、日本株にも調整圧力
が生じ得ると指摘しており、相応の株式を保有する金融機関にとってもリスクとなる。 
さらに、日銀によると、世界の株式市場に投資するヘッジファンドでは、AI関連銘柄の保有割
合が高まっている。マクロ戦略を採るヘッジファンドでは、個別株式の保有額に占める米大手ハ
イテク 7社の割合が、2019年の約14％から2026年には約23％に上昇した（注3） 。こうしたファ
ンドのなかには、借り入れやデリバティブを活用して投資規模を拡大し、株式だけでなく国債な
どにも投資するものがある。AI関連株が急落すれば、損失の拡大や追加の証拠金請求などを受け
て、株式以外の投資ポジションも縮小する可能性がある。 
この影響が及び得る市場の一つが、日本の国債市場だ。ヘッジファンドを含む海外投資家は、
日本国債の現物と先物、金利スワップなどの価格差を利用した裁定取引を拡大させているとみら
れる。日銀によると、国債現物の対顧客取引に占める海外投資家の割合は、2015年の約3割から
2025年には6割強に上昇した（注4）。 
AI関連株の下落をきっかけにファンドがレバレッジを縮小すれば、国債関連のポジション解消
が進み、債券価格や金利の変動につながる可能性がある。日銀は、海外投資家による取引の増加
が市場の流動性や価格形成に寄与する場合がある一方、海外市場のショックが証拠金の請求など
をきっかけにポジション解消を引き起こし、日本の金融市場に影響を与えやすくなっている可能
性を指摘している。

（3） 実体経済を通じた経路 
日銀はストレステストで、ハイパースケーラーなどのAI関連投資が失速した場合、AI関連産業
の比重が高いアジア経済が大きく減速すると想定している。日本でも、情報通信機械、はん用・
生産用機械、電気機械などのAI関連産業を中心に経済が減速する。企業収益の悪化は、アジア向
けを中心とする貸出の信用コスト増加を通じて、金融機関に跳ね返る。 
AI関連投資を巡る三つの波及経路は相互に関連している。設備投資の減速による企業収益の悪
化は、株価の下落や信用リスクの上昇にもつながり得る。日本と海外NBFIの結びつきは、すでに
相当な規模に達している。日本の銀行による海外NBFIへの投融資残高は、2008年 3月末の8兆円
から 2026年6月末には126兆円に増加した。海外の投資ファンド等による日本の株式・債券への
投資残高も、同期間に111兆円から408兆円へ拡大している（資料2）。このうち株式が355兆円
を占め、債券は53兆円にとどまる（注5） 。いずれもAI関連に限った金額ではないが、海外の金融
市場で発生したショックが日本に波及する経路の広がりを示している。

### Page 5

5/8

資料 2 日本の金融部門と海外ノンバンクの結びつき

（注）左図は国内銀行等による海外ノンバンクへの投融資残高。右図は、日銀がIMFの部門別対外証券投資データなどを用いて
試算した、海外主体による日本向け証券投資残高のうち「投資ファンド等」の値で、ディーラー・ブローカーやファイナンス会
社などを含む。海外ノンバンク全体の日本向け証券投資残高は 507兆円（2026年 6月末）。 
（出所）日本銀行「金融システムレポート」（2026年 10月号）図表Ⅴ-2-2（原資料はBIS、IMF、U.S. Department of the Treasury、
日本銀行）をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

4. 複合ショックにも耐性を示す日本の金融機関 
前章で整理した銀行融資、金融市場、実体経済の三つの波及経路に沿って、日本の金融機関へ
の影響をみていこう。日銀は銀行105行、信用金庫247庫を対象に、2028年度末までの財務状況
を試算するマクロ・ストレステストを実施した。本稿で確認する「海外金利上昇シナリオplus」
では、AI関連投資の収益性に対する期待の縮小に加え、内外のNBFIによる資産売却などを通じて
金融市場の混乱が増幅される状況を想定した。 
まず、銀行融資を通じた波及経路を確認する。海外のデータセンター事業者やプライベートク
レジット（PC）ファンド向けの貸出が傷むというシナリオを置いた。両者に対する貸出の信用コ
スト率が最大約10ポイント悪化する想定だ。もっとも、国際統一基準行の信用コスト増加に占め
る海外ファンド・データセンター向けの寄与は限定的だった。 
金融市場を通じた経路では、株価や債券価格の大幅な下落を想定した。米国ではAI関連銘柄に
ITバブル崩壊後の下落率を当てはめるなどして、米国株価がベースラインから最大約50％下落す
るとした。日本株も最大約40％の下落を見込む。また、NBFIによるショックの増幅を織り込み、
米国のBBB格社債の信用スプレッドは最大約1,100ベーシスポイント拡大する。日米の長期金利も
ベースラインより1.5ポイント上昇する。こうした株価と債券価格の同時下落によって、金融機
関の有価証券評価損益などが悪化する。また、海外金利の上昇に伴う外貨調達コストの増加が、
海外の資金利益を押し下げる。 
実体経済を通じた経路では、ハイパースケーラーなどのAI関連投資の失速によって、関連産業

### Page 6

6/8 
の比重が高いアジア経済が大きく減速すると想定した。日銀の試算では、アジアの実質GDPはベー
スラインから最大約7％下振れする。日本でも関連製造業を中心に企業収益が悪化し、銀行の信用
コスト増加につながる。今回のシナリオには原油高などの影響も含まれており、信用コストの増
加には国内向け貸出の寄与が大きい。 
こうした複合的なショックを想定しても、日銀の試算では金融機関の自己資本比率は全体とし
て規制水準を上回った。2028年度末の国際統一基準行の自己資本比率は、ベースラインの12.8％
から 8.9％に低下する（資料3）。

資料 3 マクロ・ストレステストにおける 2028年度末の自己資本比率

（注）対象は銀行 105行と信用金庫 247庫。「金融調整シナリオ」は、内外の金融市場や実体経済にリーマンショック並みの大幅
な調整が生じる想定。「海外金利上昇シナリオplus」は、内外金利の上昇、原油価格の上昇、AI関連投資の収益性に対する
期待の縮小などが同時に生じ、ノンバンク部門によるショックの増幅も織り込んだ想定。 
（出所）日本銀行「金融システムレポート」（2026年 10月号）図表Ⅵ-2-5、Ⅵ-2-7をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

この水準はリーマンショック並みの市場調整を想定した「金融調整シナリオ」の10.1％を下回
る。今回のシナリオの影響の大きさが分かる。一方、国内基準行の銀行は10.4％から8.8％、信
用金庫は14.0％から13.2％への低下にとどまった。国内基準行では、金融調整シナリオ（銀行
8.6％、信用金庫12.6％）の方が低下幅は大きい。 
日銀は、AI関連投資への期待の縮小などがNBFIを通じて増幅される複合的なショックに対して
も、「金融システムの安定性は全体として維持される」と評価している。ただ、試算結果は業態ご
との平均値で、個々の金融機関の自己資本比率にはばらつきがある点には留意が必要だろう。

5. おわりに 
日銀のストレステストでは、AI関連投資への期待の縮小やNBFIを通じたショックの増幅を織り
込んでも、日本の金融機関は全体として相応の耐性を備えているとの結果となった。一方、AI関

### Page 7

7/8 
連企業間の循環的な取引が、損失を増幅させる可能性にも注目したい。 
現在のAI関連企業の資金調達の特徴として、資金と売上が企業間で相互に結びつく関係があげ
られる。この循環的な取引を拙稿「AIと金融市場、強まる相互依存～IMFの国際金融安定性報告書
から読み解く新たなリスク～」で指摘した。本稿の2章でも述べた通り、大手企業が財務基盤の
相対的に弱いAI企業に出資し、出資先が見返りとして大手企業のサービスや半導体を将来購入す
ることを約束する仕組みだ。IMFのGFSRによると、約6,500億ドルの債務のうち約3,200億ドルは
出資先が負っており、そこには出資元の保証付きの債務やオフバランスの債務も含まれる。出資
先の経営が悪化すれば、出資元の将来の売上高が減少すると同時に、出資や融資の価値も損なわ
れる可能性がある。IMFはこれを「誤方向リスク（wrong-way risk） 」と呼び、循環的な取引が損
失を増幅するリスクを評価するよう監督当局に求めている。 
筆者は、日本の金融機関もこの構造と無縁ではないと考える。データセンター事業者は、ハイ
パースケーラーとの長期リース契約を裏付けに、銀行などから資金を調達している。IMFによる
と、循環的な取引の出資を主導しているのはハイパースケーラーだ。出資先のAI企業の経営が悪
化すれば、取引相手となるハイパースケーラーの将来の売上高が下振れし、データセンターの需
要見通しにも影響が及ぶ可能性がある。そうなれば、ハイパースケーラーとのリース契約を裏付
けとする日本の大手行のデータセンター向け融資も、間接的に影響を受ける可能性がある。表面
上は信用力の高いハイパースケーラーとの契約に見えても、その先に循環的な取引が組み込まれ
ていれば、リスクは見かけより高いのではないか。 
日本の金融機関のデータセンター向けやファンド向けの貸出は、現時点では海外貸出の一部に
とどまる。それでも、損失が特定の企業群にとどまらず、連鎖的に広がっていくリスクには目を
配っておきたい。AI関連投資が持続的な収益を生み出せるかに加え、企業間の出資と取引の結び
つきが、銀行やNBFIの資金供給を通じて金融システムにどう波及するのか。その把握には、個々
の融資先の信用力だけでなく、取引の連鎖全体を見る視点が必要になるだろう。

以 上

【注釈】

2) 日銀「金融システムレポート」 （2026年 10月号）図表Ⅴ-2-9をもとに筆者が概算。対象は

まない。

高はディーラー対顧客取引分（顧客別にみた購入額と売却額の合計）で、国庫短期証券は含ま

## Document 274: Compelling risk-reward in US 10-year bonds

Source PDF: all report pdf/Compelling risk-reward in US 10-year bonds.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-09-28
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Compelling risk-reward in 
US 10-year bonds  
Summary 
Event: The US 10 -year government bond yield has surged 
past 5.0% to a 19-year high and has now entered our three -
month forecast range of 5.0-5.25%. At the time of writing, the 
10-year yield is trading at 5.2%, its highest since June 2007’s 
high of 5.32%. The next resistance levels are the 2002 high 
of 5.47% and the 2001 high of 5.53%.  
We view the risk/reward for the US 10-year government 
bond after the recent yield spike as  compelling and we 
are initiating a long opportunistic idea at the current 
yield. As the table on the right shows, if the 10 -year yield 
rises to 6%, investors would face less than 1% in negative 
total returns over a one-year period, whereas if the yield falls 
below 5.0%, they would gain at least 6% in total returns.  
While we continue to prefer the 3-7-year bond maturity range 
for income generation (the US 5-year bond yield is also 
above 5%), and broadly prefer corporate over government 
bonds amid solid corporate fundamentals, the US 10 -year 
government bond yield has finally reached reasonable levels 
for investors looking for slightly longer maturities. 
Rationale: We expect the US 10-year government bond yield 
to ease to 4.75-5.0% over a 12-month period due to: 
Easing oil prices and inflation over next 12 months: While 
the yield is likely to remain elevated in the next three months 
due to elevated oil prices caused by geopolitical uncertainty, 
heavy AI investment-driven bond issuance and a resilient US 
economy, it is likely to ease towards the 4.75 -5.0% range 
over 12  months as oil prices moderate either due to a 
resolution in the Middle East or a rerouting of Middle East 
energy supplies and as the impact of tariffs fade.  
We expect s hallower Fed rate hikes than market s: We 
expect a total of 50bps of Fed rate hikes by June 2027, before 
a cut in H2 as US inflation eventually eases towards the Fed’s 
2% target. This  rate trajectory is shallower than c. 95bps of 
further hikes priced by markets over the next 12 months. 
 The benchmark US 10-year government bond yield has 
risen to its highest since 2007, offering medium-term 
investors a compelling risk-reward trade-off 
US 10-year government bond yield and previous highs

offers investors compelling risk-reward trade off

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 28 September 2026

Investor positioning adjustment: In the very near-term, the 
US 10 -year bond yield has room to move lower as hedge 
funds using systematic strategies (CTAs) are extremely short 
US government bonds, raising the chance of short -covering 
if upcoming jobs and inflation data miss estimates. 
A shallow Fed hiking cycle  
We expect the shallowest Fed hiking cycle in almost 
three decades. This should enable the US 10-year yield to 
ease towards 4.75 -5.0% over a 12 -month period. Following 
this month’s increase, we forecast two further 25bp hikes by 
June 2027, followed by a cut in H2 2027. Markets price at 
least three more hikes by June and a strong probability of a 
fourth by September. Our projected 75bp s of total hikes this 
cycle compares with 525bps of hikes in 2022–23, 225bps in 
2015–18, 425bps in 2004–06 and 175bp in 1999–2000. The 
last smaller mid-cycle move was a single 25bps hike in 1997. 
Why we expect a more cautious Fed 
The latest hike restored the Fed’s credibility after renewed 
oil-price pressures, elevated inflation and a balanced labour 
market prompted a unanimous policy vote. However, the 
following factors argue against aggressive tightening: 
Supply-driven inflation: Energy costs and AI-infrastructure 
constraints, rather than excess demand, are driving prices. 
Higher rates cannot repair supply and may weaken growth. 
Labour is no longer the main inflation source:  Labour 
supply and demand are better balanced, wage growth has 
moderated and unemployment is near full employment. AI 
further weakens the traditional Phillips Curve relationship 
between unemployment and inflation. 
Disinflation should resume:  Energy pressures may fade 
after the US mid-term elections, while the one-off inflationary 
effects of tariffs should diminish after 2025–26. 
AI raises the neutral rate , but dampens inflation:  
Investment and productivity may lift the neutral rate ( r*), the 
rate consistent with stable inflation and full employment, 
while efficiency gains limit wage-price spirals. 
Markets are already tightening  financial conditions : 
Higher bond yields have raised borrowing costs and 
restrained activity, doing part of the Fed’s work. 
The cycle began from a higher base:  The Fed funds rate 
started at 3.75%, versus 0.25% in the previous two cycles 
and 1% before that, reducing additional tightening required. 
Monetary transmission has weakened:  Wealthier 
households drive more spending but are less rate -sensitive, 
while lower -income consumers are already constrained. 
Further hikes may , therefore, curb demand less effectively 
while causing disproportionate harm to low-income families. 
 We expect a shallower Fed rate hiking cycle compared 
with market expectations

### Page 3

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 28 September 2026

Small business conditions are deteriorating:  Higher 
financing and energy costs are weakening sentiment, as 
reflected in the latest Beige Book . The renewed 
underperformance of US small capitalisation (S&P 600) and 
mid capitalisation (S&P 400) equity indices  versus the large 
cap S&P 500 index over the past couple of months also show 
rising pressure on small businesses. 
Fiscal and financial -stability risks are rising:  Aggressive 
Fed tightening would increase US debt-servicing costs as the 
US Treasury seeks cheaper funding through shorter-maturity 
issuance, while potentially amplifying financial-system stress. 
Upside risk to bond yields 
Our view of lower bond yields carries the following risks: 
Persistent inflation:  Repeated supply shocks, strong AI -
related investment demand and tighter immigration controls 
could keep inflation above target and force steeper Fed rate 
hikes. With unemployment near the Fed’s estimate of full 
employment, a sharp drop in labour supply could intensify 
wage pressures and destabilise inflation expectations . We 
note that long-term expectations remain contained for now. 
Geopolitical escalation:  An intensification of geopolitical 
conflicts after the mid -term elections could lift energy prices 
and inflation. However, a Democrat -controlled or divided 
Congress may constrain US foreign-policy initiatives through 
tighter budgets, while Republicans could turn less supportive 
as attention shifts to the 2028 presidential race. 
Higher neutral rate: AI investments could raise the neutral 
rate (r*), turning current monetary policy settings 
accommodative. This could necessitate further rate hikes. 
However, the Fed has barely raised its nominal neutral rate 
estimate, lifting it to 3.2% in September from 3.0% a year ago. 
Bottomline: The outlook for rates remains geopolitics 
dependent, with all Fed meetings live . However, based on 
our core scenario, we see the US 10-year yield settling lower 
in the 4.75-5.0% in 6-12 months. A shallow Fed hiking cycle 
due to expected disinflation offers investors a reasonably 
attractive risk-reward in US 10-year government bonds. 
US 30-year government bonds , on the other hand, may 
continue to see elevated yields as the US fiscal deficit is 
unlikely to have improved next year, and may look much 
worse. As a result, “term premium” may keep the ultra -long 
end elevated. Short of some unorthodox policy to artifici ally 
cap them lower, the 30-year yield is likely to continue to drift 
higher towards the 5.5-5.75% range into H2 2027. 
— Jonathan Liang, CIO for Fixed Income and FX   
Rajat Bhattacharya, Senior Investment Strategist 
Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist

## Document 414: What lessons can we learn to keep European industry competitive from the measures taken by Germany, as the cost of electricity continues to turn the screw_ (2026-10-22)

Source PDF: all report pdf/What lessons can we learn to keep European industry competitive from the measures taken by Germany, as the cost of electricity continues to turn the screw_ (2026-10-22).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
WHAT LESSONS CAN WE LEARN TO KEEP EUROPEAN INDUSTRY COMPETITIVE FROM
THE MEASURES TAKEN BY GERMANY, AS THE COST OF ELECTRICITY CONTINUES TO
TURN THE SCREW?
Marianne Mueller and Pascal Devaux
issue 19
21 October 2026
The 2022 energy crisis clearly highlighted the issue of electricity prices in Europe — and, more broadly, the issue of energy prices. As
a result, European manufacturers’ competitiveness has been severely undermined when compared with the United States and China.

By reigniting tensions over electricity prices, the conflict that began in the Middle East in late February 2026 further underscores the
continent’s vulnerability to fossil fuel imports, despite the expansion of renewable energy. Faced with an environment of potential sharp
and sudden price rises, European governments do not have the same room for manoeuvre.
While the additional costs borne by European manufacturers are mainly linked to the share of gas in national electricity mixes, we will

first illustrate that, in Germany, grid operation and maintenance costs, as well as taxation, are also contributing significantly to these
costs.
We will then explore the solutions proposed by the German government under the new European measures regulating state aid to
electricity-intensive industries.
Finally, our estimates will show that, while these measures make Germany more competitive against some European countries, they
do not place it on an equal competitive footing with the United States or China. Nevertheless, Germany has made significant progress

in decarbonising and making its electricity mix more flexible from a European perspective. This should help to ensure that electricity costs
are reduced in the medium term.

### Page 2

2
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoInsight 19 / 21 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
worldWHAT LESSONS CAN WE LEARN TO KEEP EUROPEAN INDUSTRY COMPETITIVE FROM THE MEA-SURES TAKEN BY GERMANY, AS THE COST OF ELECTRICITY CONTINUES TO TURN THE SCREW?
GAS: A KEY FACTOR IN THE ADDITIONAL ELECTRICITY COSTS
FACED BY EUROPEAN INDUSTRY
European manufacturers are still paying significantly higher elec -
tricity costs than their American and Chinese competitors. In 2019,

there was a 30% difference between the prices charged to European
electricity-intensive industries (corresponding to the lower end of the
industrial price range) and the average US industrial price. In 2025,
it stood at nearly 100% according to the IEA (2026) 1, after peaking at
an average of 148% between 2022 and 2023. There is currently a 50%
difference with their Chinese competitors2.
The disruption to Russian gas supplies since 2022 is behind the rising

gas prices, particularly as the EU has had to turn to liquefied natural
gas3 (LNG), which is more expensive than gas transported via pipe -
line and, above all, exposes Europe to global gas market conditions.
The growth of LNG has, in fact, intensified competition between the
major consumption regions, primarily Europe and Asia. European and
Asian prices now tend to move more in tandem (Chart 1). However, in
China, the prevalence of coal — a widely available local resource —

and a more regulated electricity price help to keep industrial prices
lower. At the same time, the United States enjoys plentiful domestic
gas, which is produced at a low cost, driven by the sharp rise in shale
oil and gas production over the past decade. By contrast, since 2022,
Europe has been more exposed to the cost of imported gas (90% of its
total consumption, according to Eurostat), while the United States and
China have enjoyed plentiful domestic resources.

Fluctuations and the level of gas prices are passed on to Eu -
ropean electricity prices via the ‘merit order’ marginal pricing
mechanism, with the wholesale price determined by the margi -
nal cost of the last (and most expensive) electricity source called
upon to meet demand.
As a result, gas plays a decisive role, with gas-fired power sta -
tions setting the price in around 40% of cases in Europe4, with si-
gnificant variations depending on national electricity mixes. It is

close to this average in Germany, below it in Spain (26%) and
France (17%), and above it in Italy (73%) 5 in 2025 6 7. Therefore,
electricity prices are still sensitive to fluctuations in gas prices,
even when electricity is not generated from gas (Chart 2). This
pass-through is particularly pronounced when demand rises
and requires the use of more expensive sources, or when supply
constraints arise (for example, during extreme heatwaves that
reduce wind-power generation or disrupt the cooling of nuclear

power plants).
1 Prices – Electricity 2026 – Analysis – IEA
2 The difference may be even larger for other manufacturers who do not receive the same support as electricity-intensive sectors.
3 Imports of Russian gas (via pipeline and LNG) accounted for around 45% of total European imports in 2021, compared with 14% in Q2 2026 (Bruegel, natural gas imports).
4 ACER, 2026, Key developments in EU electricity and gas markets, 2026 Monitoring report.
5 In Italy, in response to sharply rising gas prices in 2026, the government is currently negotiating a potential option of capping the prices of gas supplied to power stations in

order to limit the rise in the wholesale price of electricity. However, this can only be a temporary general mechanism, with the potentially major cost being borne by public funds
and/or households.
6 Eurelectric, 2026, Power Barometer.
7 EMBER, 2026, Latest energy shock reminds Europe of its risky reliance on gas.
8 Energy shock: 2026 vs. 2022 dashboard – 2nd October 2026 – Economic Studies – BNP Paribas
However, for the time being, the 2026 energy crisis is having a far

smaller effect on electricity prices than in 2022. On the one hand, the
2026 peak reached by the European TTF benchmark is still well below
that recorded in 20228.
In addition, there has been significant progress in decarbonising Eu -
ropean electricity mixes since 2022, which has helped to mitigate, on
average, the pass-through of gas prices to electricity prices.

EcoInsight 19 / 21 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com

SOME EUROPEAN COUNTRIES HAVE MANAGED TO STAY
COMPETITIVE, BUT GERMANY IS STILL THE EXCEPTION
Electricity prices partly vary due to countries’ electricity mixes
(Chart 3). Electricity-intensive manufacturers in countries where the
share of fossil energy is high face higher costs (Poland, Italy and Ire -
land) (Chart 4). Conversely, countries with a less carbon-intensive mix

(particularly France and Spain in recent times) are less directly ex -
posed to these fluctuations, with costs closer to the United States and
China (Chart 5).
The situation in Germany is paradoxical, as, despite progressively
greening its electricity mix and wholesale electricity prices (averaging
90 EUR/MWh over the 2023–2025 period) and average electricity costs
(across all industrial sectors) in line with the European average, the most
energy-intensive industries face higher costs than the European average

(and even more so compared with international competitors). This is
mainly due to grid costs and a tax burden above EU standards (Chart 6).
9 Eurostat data are used to compare prices with the rest of the EU. However, given that electricity prices are not uniform for businesses at a European level, there may be differences
between Eurostat and national sources. Jäger P ., 2025, “Cautionary tale or model for Europe? How member states should react to Germany’s new electricity subsidy”, Hertie School,

Jacques Delors Centre.
10 P . Devaux, 2026, “European Union: low carbon transition and energy sovereignty, a path fraught with obstacles”
In the first half of 2026, for the most electricity-intensive industries,
the difference compared with the European average was 9%, based on
the prices excluding taxes and levies provided by Eurostat. This gap is
mainly due to the cost of accessing the grid. If all taxes and levies are
included, the difference rises to 21% (Chart 7)9.

EUROPE’S APPROACH OF ADOPTING BOTH STRUCTURAL
PROGRAMMES AND TARGETED SUPPORT MEASURES
European programmes to support the low-carbon transition pursue
objectives relating to climate action (reducing greenhouse gas emis -
sions), sovereignty10 (increasing energy self-sufficiency) and competi -
tiveness (reducing the volatility and costs associated with fossil fuels).
The RePowerEU programme, which was launched in 2022 in response
to the energy crisis, primarily aims to reduce imports of Russian gas

and, more generally, to accelerate the low-carbon transition. Although
Europe has reduced its dependence on imports of Russian fossil fuels,
there has still not been enough progress around the energy transition
and cross-border connectivity. This is due to governance constraints

and struggles in implementing the programme in practice within each
Member State11.
State aid is still the main vehicle for this. In response to the 2021–
2022 energy crisis, the European Union temporarily relaxed the rules
on this type of aid in order to support businesses facing rising energy
prices. Any intervention of this kind, provided that it is selective and

could affect trade within the single market, must be authorised in ad -
vance by the European Commission in principle. Since June 2025, the
Clean Industrial Deal State Aid Framework (CISAF) has provided a more
sustainable framework for these kinds of interventions. In particular,
this framework, which runs until the end of 2030, authorises Member
States to grant temporary aid to electricity-intensive businesses to off-
set the cost of electricity. The aid must be targeted and come with

decarbonisation commitments12. Against the backdrop of the crisis in
the Middle East, the temporary METSAF scheme, adopted in April 2026,
helped (in relation to measures falling under the CISAF) to increase the
proportion of additional costs offset by governments from 50% to 70%,
thereby offering additional support to the most at-risk sectors.
GERMANY IS STILL SUFFERING FROM INSUFFICIENT IN-
VESTMENT IN ITS ELECTRICITY NETWORKS, WHICH THE
AID SCHEMES ARE ATTEMPTING TO OFFSET

The issue of electricity costs is a particularly hot issue in Germany,
given the importance of industry to the economy (around 20% of va -
lue added, one of the highest levels in Europe) and its exposure to
international competition. Furthermore, the delay in modernising
electricity infrastructure (only 22.6% of the high-voltage lines planned
for 2025 had actually been completed by that date) is causing bottle -
necks between the production areas in the north and the consump -

11 European Court of Auditors, 2026, “Implementation of the REPowerEU plan must be stepped up”.
12 It applies to the most electricity-intensive activities, which are seeing their competitiveness threatened by international competition, and follows the 4x50 principle: 50% of
electricity consumption is eligible for a subsidy; Of the eligible proportion, the subsidy may not exceed 50% of the wholesale price;
The final wholesale price paid by the company must not be less than €50/MWh;

50% of the aid received must be reinvested in decarbonisation (renewable energy generation, storage and improvements in energy efficiency); Around 90 sectors are covered by this
reform, accounting for approximately 2,000 companies, including in the chemical, steel and paper industries. The scheme, which is strictly regulated, will run for just three years
(from 1st January 2026 to the end of 2028) at an estimated total cost of between EUR 3 and 5 bn, financed by the special Climate and Transformation Fund.

13 Bundesnetzagentur Monitoring Report (2015) and Bundesnetzagentur Monitoring Report (2025). These costs mainly cover the costs of physical infrastructure; operational,
regulatory and compliance costs; and costs associated with using the transmission and distribution network.
14 The Industriestrompreis (industrial electricity price) is the public subsidy mechanism aiming to reduce the price of electricity for some industries.
15 The indirect carbon cost compensation mechanism is a government support measure for electricity-intensive industries, which aims to offset the cost of carbon-emission

allowances for energy producers.
tion centres in the south. As a result, the costs of accessing services
have increased fivefold over 10 years (rising from EUR 1 bn in 2014
to EUR 5.2 bn in 2024 13). At the same time, as we have already seen,
taxation and grid charges are hitting industry hard, with levies on large
consumers far exceeding the European average despite some support
mechanisms being put in place for the most electricity-intensive com-

panies (such as the Bandlastprivileg measure to reduce grid tariffs).
As a result, German industrial production has suffered in particular
due to rising energy prices in recent years, including electricity, with
electricity-intensive industries being especially affected (Chart 8).
In order to cope with these higher electricity costs than the European
average, Germany has been using European support schemes to reduce
the cost of electricity for businesses, particularly since the Merz go -

vernment took office in May 2025. The main scheme — the Industries-
trompreis14, made possible by the CISAF — has been approved by the
European Commission for the 2026–2028 period. While it is less bene-
ficial than the indirect carbon cost (ICC) offsetting included in electri -
city prices15, it has a broader scope, meaning that businesses ineligible
for the ICC can benefit from it. Other measures complement this targe-
ted support by addressing other components of the electricity bill. As a

result, the electricity tax has been set at the European minimum for
manufacturing sectors, while the gas-storage tax has been abolished.
The government is also subsidising grid fees, which should halve them.
Overall, according to our estimates based on 2024 prices, the final
price of electricity for these industries could fall by around a quarter
(to 116 EUR/MWh for industries with annual consumption of between
70 and 150 GWh, compared with a European average of 123 EUR/MWh)

(Chart 9). Germany’s advantage over Central and Eastern European
countries, where electricity costs are generally already higher, looks
set to increase.

However, even when taking all of the subsidies into account, the price
of electricity in Germany would remain higher than the levels seen in
France, Spain or Portugal (and even more so in the United States and
China).
FISCAL MEASURES: LIMITED ROOM FOR MANOEUVRE
There are a number of limitations to these European schemes. For
the country implementing them, lower electricity costs mean that

its industry becomes more competitive against industries from other
EU countries, but this does very little to address the disadvantage that
it faces compared with the United States and China. Therefore, this
measure will unlikely be enough to counteract the loss of market share
seen vis-à-vis the United States and China. In addition, the support
schemes are restrictive (conditional on investment in the low-carbon
transition) and are transitional. Finally, their cost is partly passed on

to the public purse or to consumers who do not receive this support
(in Germany, the cost of the new energy price support measures is
estimated at EUR 14.3 bn in 2026, or 0.3% of GDP16).
Some European manufacturers have proposed abolishing or reducing
the carbon tax as a solution to getting electricity prices down quickly.
In Germany, this tax accounted for 14% of the electricity price in the
first half of 2025, which is higher than the EU average (11%). However,

this proposal is already covered by the indirect carbon cost offsetting
mechanism for electricity-intensive industries exposed to carbon al -
lowance prices. In addition, while adjustments are being made — no -
tably to slow the rate at which allowances are reduced — it seems
highly unlikely that the scheme will be significantly challenged. It is
one of the cornerstones of Europe’s policy to combat climate change,
which has been effective thus far, as shown by the falling greenhouse

gas emissions from industry. More broadly, the gradual introduction of
a carbon border adjustment mechanism (CBAM) should mitigate com-
petition distortions caused by environmental practices17.
IN GERMANY AND THE REST OF EUROPE, HEAVY INVEST -
MENT IS DRIVING UP ELECTRICITY BILLS IN THE SHORT
TERM
In response to the challenge of the low-carbon transition and indus -
trial competitiveness, European governments must strike a balance
between decarbonising the electricity mix and ensuring a supply of

energy that is flexible enough (particularly from controllable sources)
to guarantee stable electricity systems. The integration of an increa -
sing proportion of intermittent renewable energy sources requires
massive investment in transmission and storage infrastructure (statio-
nary batteries). This expenditure, which places a long-term burden on
grid costs, reduces the scope for support schemes (particularly public
ones) to address competitiveness gaps. In Germany, the cost of the

transition is estimated at between EUR 122 bn, according to the go -
vernment (2.8% of GDP), and EUR 200 bn, according to the DIHK18 (4.6%
of GDP), by 2030–2035. If we take the ECB’s assumption19 that govern-
ments will cover around 17% of these investments, the annual public
16 The Industriestrompreis is estimated at around EUR 1.3 bn per year, the subsidy for network charges is projected to amount to EUR 6.5 bn in 2026, the abolition of the gas sur -

charge around EUR 3.4 bn, and the reduction in the electricity tax nearly EUR 3 bn per year. Furthermore, the ICC had cost EUR 2.3 bn in 2024 for costs incurred in 2023.

expenditure burden of the transition in Germany would be between
0.5 and 0.8 percentage points of GDP by 2030. However, this budgetary
pressure comes at the same time as three other budgetary increases:
rising social expenditure linked to an ageing population; the necessary
increase to defence budgets to meet NATO’s target of 3.5% of GDP by
2029; and the rising cost of debt-servicing20.
Germany has already made significant progress in maximising its use

of renewable energy, putting it at the head of the pack in Europe. The
country generates 20% of Europe’s renewable electricity (solar and
wind) and accounts for 30% of installed storage capacity (BESS) 21, far
ahead of other European countries (ranking third globally). However,
despite this momentum, Germany’s dependence on gas has not yet
decreased. As a matter of fact, it is only partially offsetting the planned
closure of coal-fired power stations and the phasing out of nuclear

reactors. Furthermore, the intermittent nature of solar and wind power
could prolong this dependence in the short and medium term. In order
to ensure that the German electricity system is flexible, new invest -
ments in gas-fired power plants, supported by public subsidies, are
planned22.

SUBSIDIES ARE HELPING TO ADDRESS THE EMERGENCY
CAUSED BY ENERGY CRISES WITHOUT RESTORING THE
INTERNATIONAL COMPETITIVENESS OF ENERGY-INTEN-
SIVE INDUSTRIES
In response to the impact of energy crises on industrial competitive -
ness, European governments are finding themselves having to strike a
delicate balance between providing short-term support to industries

exposed to increased international competition and continuing — if
not, accelerating — energy transition policies which will result in hi -
gher costs for electricity-network users for several years to come.
Beyond direct economic support measures, which are difficult to sus -
tain long term, four priority areas are emerging:
1/ Demonstrating flexibility in implementing European directives in
order to factor in national specificities and the differing timeframes

between energy crises (which can lead to irreversible industrial conse-
quences) and reforms (which have a longer-term horizon). The relaxa-
tion of the European carbon tax mechanism 23 appears to be a step
towards this.
2/ Continuing efforts to decarbonise the electricity mix, which are still
the main vehicle for sustainably reducing electricity prices for Euro -
pean industries in the medium and long term.
3/ Optimising the use of electricity networks on the demand side, in

particular through smart grids, a flagship element of the Commission’s
COM (2026) 600 reform presented last July24.
4/ Coordinating European electricity networks in order to harness the
complementarities between national systems.
Article completed on 19 October 2026

卡達仍有 199億里亞爾的經常帳盈餘 ，累積過去四
季經常帳占 GDP比重仍達 13.5%，現今外匯存底足
以彌補3月的貿易逆差，並可維持４年以上。這些數
據都顯示戰爭對中東國家的經濟及對外償債能力確
實已帶來短線壓力 ，但過去累積的龐大緩衝使其具
備良好因應能力 ，信用違約交換 (CDS)目前並未因
定價油氣供應長期中斷而大幅攀升 ，今年雖持續發
債但債務壓力並不高 ，IMF更預期2027年中東國家
經濟將成長6.3%。然中東國家對能源出口依賴度仍
高，能源出口是否長期受阻則是後續觀察重點。
重點摘要
• 沙烏地阿拉伯負債占 GDP比
重約 35%低於全球新興國家

均值 ，2027年預算目標為將
赤佔 GDP 比 率 從2026 年的

不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行並不負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資之風險，並就投
資結果自行負責。未經本公司許可，本資料及訊息不得逕行抄錄、翻印或另作派發。本行以上市場資訊與分析，不涉及特定投資標的之建議。
重點摘要 《經濟強勁帶來的高利率無損新興債利差穩步收斂 》
財政收支並非驅動新興主權信用利差的唯一因素 ，
總體經濟環境 、市場流動性均牽動利差走勢 ，因此
雖然自7月以來沙烏地阿拉伯等中東國家信用利差又
再擴大，但也大概在3年均值附近並不算高。以歷史
經驗來看，BB級主權國家利差通常對財政惡化程度
反應較大 ，可能是因為該評等僅低於投資等級一級 ，
財政變化容易牽動投資人對升等至投資級的預期與
投資部位的調整 ，而在投資等級的主權國家中 ，則
以匈牙利和波蘭這些債務負擔較重的主權債利差較
易受財政問題影響。
油價和利率是近期2項重要觀察變數，也牽動利差走
勢，不過由於新興主權債指數中約5成的組成為大宗
商品出口國、3成為石油出口國，反而能受惠油價上

漲，油價上漲時與利差走勢敏感度也較油價下跌時
低；然而 ，更重要的是高油價對通膨和政策應對的
二階影響 ，將導致高利率長時間延續的可能性並最
終衝擊實體經濟 ，進一步限制石油出口國利差收窄 。
以長期趨勢來看 ，利率水準的絕對值高低不必然影
響新興債利差 ，利率走勢反而和信用利差呈現小幅
負相關，利率走升反映的若是強勁經濟成長而非能
源供給衝擊 ，此時往往能伴隨信用利差收窄 ，反而
需留意的是利率波動放大及利率的大幅重新定價 ，
這才是牽動利差波動加大的主因 。目前總體經濟環
境部分或可與2004~2007年相比擬，當時在強勁成
長的背景下利率長時間在4%~5.3%高位震盪，但新
興主要貨幣主權債利差仍可收斂至 134點(此波低點
189點)，若經濟成長能在現今高油價和高利率環境
下持續強勁，利差則可望保持穩定。
重點摘要
• BB級主權債及債務負擔沈重
的投資級主權債利差較容易
受財政問題干擾
• 新興主權債指數中約 5成為石
油出口國 ，降低油價上漲對
利差走勢的敏感性
3
即使油價上升，新興債利差與油價也少見正相關

資結果自行負責。未經本公司許可，本資料及訊息不得逕行抄錄、翻印或另作派發。本行以上市場資訊與分析，不涉及特定投資標的之建議。
重點摘要 《景氣及企業獲利續成長 ，企業債信改善趨勢延續 》
三大信評機構持續認可新興市場四大區域企業信用
評等的改善，3Q26新興市場企業債淨升等規模大幅
提高至760億美元，其中獲上調規模達到910億美元、
創下2025年第四季以來的最高水平 ，也已是連續第
5個季度改善。
3Q26新興市場4大區域評等調升數量均高於下調 ，
其中以亞洲改善動能最為顯著 ，主要貢獻來自於亞
洲金融業 ，全年升評 /降評比率已來到 1.8倍，逆轉
2019年降評多於升評的劣勢；拉美企業仍是新興市
場中違約率最高的區域 ，降評債券數量與其他區域
比也確實較高 ，但降評企業家數已是過去 10年來最
低，加上阿根廷主權債信獲上調帶動企業債 (主要是
國有企業)信評調升，拉美航空業者如南美航空集團

(LATAM Airlines)在油價維持高位環境下仍於3Q26
獲信評機構調升至 BB+，在企業債信分化環境下仍
有亮點 ，因此第三季拉美企業淨評級上調規模達
370億美元，反而創下2025年第三季以來的最佳季
度表現 ，全年共有 234家拉美企業信評得調升 。新
歐中東地區債信今年算是少數債信惡化的區域 ，很
大部分是受到戰爭及高油價影響所致 ，不過奈及利
亞和南非等主權評級上調仍帶來正向效果 ，梯瓦製
藥(Teva)評等更是上調至投資等級，帶來了120億美
元的貢獻，部分抵消地緣政治風險帶來的影響。
強勁的獲利成長持續支持新興企業穩健基本面 ，有
限的違約率輔以韌性十足的總經環境 、以及活躍但
不過度集中的初級市場 ，均是支持債信改善的原因 ，
預期在企業獲利仍看好的情境下 ，第四季企業債信
仍將朝正向發展。

資結果自行負責。未經本公司許可，本資料及訊息不得逕行抄錄、翻印或另作派發。本行以上市場資訊與分析，不涉及特定投資標的之建議。
重點摘要 《聯準會升息年 ，新興債雖有波動但正報酬機率高 》
觀察過往聯準會升息年度 ，利率溫和走升機率相對
較高，新興債市雖然多會受到升息壓力而出現一波
跌勢，但全年度仍有較大機率收紅 ，1996年以來僅
有2018、2022這兩個升息年度新興債市全年收黑 。
不過2022年新興債市重挫很大一部分原因來自於聯
準會在短短一年時間內將基準利率自0％升至4.25%，
這種猛爆性升息與目前所預設的風險管理式的溫和
升息環境有所不同；另外 ，2018年聯準會雖只升息
四碼，但土耳其及阿根廷因美元稀缺引發流動性風
險，這2國經常帳赤字占 GDP比重當時均高於 5%，
對外融資壓力上升的同時進而爆發金融危機 。然而
現階段新興國家經常帳已普遍改善 ，土耳其經常帳

赤字已降至 2%~3%，阿根廷更轉為順差 ，基本面
與2018年有很大的差異。若扣除這兩段期間，新興
債市在聯準會升息期間波動雖可能放大 ，但在貨幣
政策方向更趨明朗後 ，走勢便會止穩反彈 ，全年平
均報酬率仍有10%以上。
至於新興市場企業債部分 ，過去因升息所造成的跌
勢約是主權債的一半 ，主要是因為企業債發行天期
多較主權要來得短 ，加上該期間獲利動能強勁 ，因
此在利率上升時期表現往往相對主權債更具韌性 。
儘管利率走勢仍存不確定性，但最終若能穩定在5%
左右，預估新興企業債全年總報酬仍可維持在3%，
除非未來出現更大的外部衝擊 ，如利率持續大幅上
升引發對經濟成長受損的擔憂 ，或AI驅動的成長模
式被重新評價 ，否則對新興債市的看法仍將維持正
向，新興主要貨幣主權債在這樣的大環境下 ，短線
雖相對承壓，但後續反彈力道也可望大於企業債。
• 新興主要貨幣主權債在聯準
會升息年度正報酬機率近8成
• 新興企業債相對抗跌 ，公債
利率若穩定在5%左右，全年
總報酬仍可維持在3%
5

東協維持整理，印度新契機浮現
分析師／王聖堯
研究範疇／東協/印度
東南亞投資攻略
出 刊 日 : 2026/10/1
熱門議題 / 投資關鍵
《東協尚未擺脫整理，美印關係改善訊號浮現》
9月MSCI東協指數下跌 2.6%，雖然全球公債殖利率
與油價走升帶來評價面壓力 ，但從股價跌幅及外資
賣超規模來看 ，本波修正仍偏向短線漲多後的技術
性調節。修正後，東協股市本益比已由高於近兩年
平均2倍標準差回落至約 1倍標準差 ，RSI亦降至40
以下，顯示市場過熱情況明顯改善 。然而在聯準會
升息及殖利率維持高檔的背景下 ，若基本面未能提
供更多上修空間 ，預期股市短期仍將維持震盪整理
格局。印度方面，9月回檔已使股價回測 3月低點，
本益比降至 19.9倍，RSI跌破30，顯示短線賣壓已

有過度反應。此外，莫迪於9月金磚峰會公開反對去
美元化，近期川普亦表態支持印歐經濟走廊 ，反映
美印經貿關係出現改善訊號 。後續可持續關注雙邊
合作進展及其對印度中長期投資影響。
市場投資展望
資產 4Q26看法 投資展望
東南亞 中性 估值及RSI已回到相對合理位置，但殖利率比原先預期更高，基本面需要看到優於預期的表現才有上調空間。
印度 正向 估值及RSI呈現超賣，近期美印雙方經貿合作釋出改善訊號，接下來可開始關注實質政策進展，及相關投資機會(投資與基礎建設概念)
• 外資9月轉為小幅賣超，呼應8月
轉中立看法 P.1
• 出口支撐成長敘事未變，但高通
膨也比預期持久 P.2

構印度樞紐角色

《外資9月轉為小幅賣超，呼應8月轉中立看法》
8月下調東協評等至中立 ，主要考量 6至8月股市累
積漲幅已大致反映第三季經濟維持穩健成長的預期 ，
在缺乏新的催化劑下 ，市場進一步上行空間受限 。
進入9月後，美國公債殖利率與國際油價同步走升，
使市場重新評估高利率與成本壓力對企業獲利的影
響，並加大非科技類股及非科技市場的修正幅度 。
不過，相較於美國公債殖利率持續創高所帶來的壓
力，東協股市 9月僅下跌 2.6%，跌幅仍屬溫和；從
資金面來看 ，外資雖轉為淨賣超 ，但賣超規模明顯
低於第二季調整期間 ，顯示本波修正較偏向漲多後
的獲利了結與資產重新配置 ，而非反映市場對區域
基本面的明顯轉弱或出現恐慌性撤資。

《出口支撐成長敘事未變 ，但高通膨也比預期持久 》
東協及印度經濟仍展現韌性，多數國家8月出口維持
雙位數成長且高於長期平均 ，與全球製造業擴張趨
勢相符 ，其中新加坡及馬來西亞受惠 AI強勁資本支
出，8月出口成長年增率逾 5成，顯示外需仍是重要
成長支撐 。強勁的出口與製造活動不僅有助支撐企
業獲利 ，也在一定程度上抵銷高利率對消費與投資
的壓力 。另一方面 ，印尼、菲律賓等先前高通膨國
家雖已進入降溫趨勢 ，但通膨回落速度仍較緩慢 ，
使央行在降息時程上維持審慎 。整體而言 ，目前東
協與印度景氣尚未出現明顯轉弱 ，但高利率環境可
能維持較市場預期更久 ，意味短期經濟成長動能將
更仰賴外需及製造業活動的支撐。

• 東協及印度出口維持強勁成

速度偏慢 ，恐使高利率環境
維持更久 ，短期成長仍須仰
賴外需支撐。

《川普表態支持印歐經濟走廊 ，重構印度樞紐角色 》
川普近期表態支持印歐經濟走廊 (IMEC)，使這項拜
登任內支持，但因加薩戰爭與區域衝突而停滯近3年
的計畫再度受到市場關注 。隨著中東地緣風險升溫 ，
紅海及荷姆茲海峽等關鍵航道不確定性提高 ，各國
對替代性能源與運輸路徑的需求同步增加 。從規劃
路線來看 ，印歐經濟走廊以印度為起點 ，串連海灣
產油國與歐洲市場 ，若計畫順利推進 ，除有助印度
取得更穩定的中東能源供給 、降低對俄羅斯石油的
依賴外 ，也將進一步鞏固其作為全球製造與供應鏈
樞紐的地位 。對印股而言 ，IMEC有望同時強化能源
安全與供應鏈地位 ，鞏固印度在全球製造的地位 ，
並為基建、工業及製造類股帶來中長期題材支撐。

重點摘要
• 川普表態支持擱置近 3年的印
歐經濟走廊重啟 ，在中東地
緣風險升溫下 ，有助印度強
化能源安全 、降低對俄油依
賴，並鞏固全球製造與供應
鏈樞紐地位。
資料來源：GettyImages

[インド]～９月インフレ率は２年弱ぶりの高い伸びに、コアインフレ率も加速するなどインフレ圧力が強まる～ 
12 日に発表された９月の消費者物価は前年同月比＋5.54％となり、 前月 （同＋4.82％） から加速
して 2024 年10 月以来の高い伸びとなった。前月比は＋0.69％と前月（同＋0.73％）からわずかに
上昇ペースが鈍化したものの、 原油価格が再び上昇していることを反映してエネルギー価格の上昇
が続いているほか、 生鮮品をはじめとする食料品価格も上昇しており、 生活必需品を中心とするイ
ンフレが続いている。食料品とエネルギーを除いたコアインフレ率は前年同月比＋4.33％となり、
前月（同＋4.16％）から加速した。前月比は＋0.40％と前月（同＋0.54％）から上昇ペースが鈍化
したものの、 エネルギー価格の上昇に伴う輸送コストの増加に加え、 金融市場でのルピー安による
輸入インフレも重なり、幅広い財で価格が上昇している。さらに、サービス物価も上昇基調で推移
しており、インフレ圧力が広がっている様子がうかがえる。

### Page 2

2/4

[オーストラリア]～大都市部を中心に雇用に変調、労働市場への参入意欲にも陰りの兆し～ 
15 日に発表された９月の失業率 （季調済） は4.7％となり、 前月 （4.7％） から２ヵ月連続で横ば
いとなった。失業者数は前月比＋0.9 万人と前月（同＋2.8 万人）から４ヵ月連続で増加した。雇
用形態別では、パートタイムの求職者数（同▲0.9 万人）が２ヵ月ぶりの減少に転じる一方、フル
タイムの求職者数 （同＋1.8 万人） は４ヵ月連続で増加しており、 失業者数全体を押し上げている。
一方、雇用者数は前月比▲3.9 万人と前月（同＋2.9 万人）から２ヵ月ぶりの減少に転じた。雇用
形態別では、フルタイムの雇用者数（同＋3.4 万人）が２ヵ月ぶりの増加に転じる一方、パートタ
イムの雇用者数（同▲7.4 万人）が減少に転じ、雇用全体を押し下げた。地域別では、資源関連産
業が集積する西オーストラリア州で雇用の増加基調が続いているものの、 最大都市シドニーを擁す
るニュー・サウス・ウェールズ州や、第２の都市メルボルンを擁するヴィクトリア州では雇用が減
少するなど、大都市部を中心に雇用環境に変化の兆しがみられる。さらに、労働力人口は前月比▲
3.0 万人と前月 （同＋5.7 万人） から２ヵ月ぶりの減少に転じた。 労働参加率も66.8％と前月 （67.0％）
から 0.2pt 低下しており、 雇用環境の悪化を背景に、 労働市場への参入意欲にも陰りが出つつある
様子がうかがえる。

[韓国]～高齢層で雇用に頭打ちの一方、若年層に持ち直しの動き、雇用環境に変化の兆し～ 
16 日に発表された９月の失業率 （季調済） は2.6％となり、 前月 （2.7％） から0.1pt 改善した。
失業者数は前月比▲4.6 万人と前月（同▲3.3 万人）から２ヵ月連続で減少しており、年代別では
20 代や 30 代のほか、50 代や 60 代以上など幅広い年代で減少する動きがみられる。一方、雇用者

動きがみられ、 中高年層の一部で労働市場からの退出が進んでいる可能性がうかがえる。 その一方、
これまで厳しい状況が続いてきた10 代や20 代など若年層の雇用環境には持ち直しの動きがみられ
ており、年代によって雇用情勢に違いが現れつつある。

[シンガポール]～世界的な AI・半導体需要の旺盛さが景気と外需を押し上げる展開が続く～ 
14 日に発表された７-９月の実質ＧＤＰ成長率 （速報値） は前年同期比＋5.4％となり、 前期 （同
＋5.9％） から伸びが鈍化した。 前期比年率ベースの成長率も＋4.42％と前期 （同＋6.15％） から鈍
化したものの、引き続きプラス成長を維持するなど、景気は堅調に推移している。分野別の動きを
みると、 世界的なＡＩ （人工知能） ・半導体関連需要の旺盛さを反映して、 電子部品関連のほか、 精
密機械関連や一般機械関連の生産は堅調に推移した。 一方、 化学製品関連や医薬品関連の生産低迷
が重しとなり、製造業全体の生産は伸び悩んだ。また、ＡＩ・半導体関連需要の旺盛さを受けた設
備投資やデータセンター関連投資の活発化に加え、 公共投資の進捗を反映して、 建設業の生産は上
振れしている。さらに、小売・卸売関連のほか、運輸・貿易関連、金融関連など幅広いサービス業
で生産が拡大しており、景気拡大を下支えしている様子がうかがえる。 
16 日に発表された９月の非石油輸出額は前年同月比＋68.4％となり、 前月 （同＋46.1％） から伸
びが加速した。前月比も＋25.53％と前月（同＋10.78％）から２ヵ月連続で拡大しており、持ち直
しの動きを強めている。 化学製品関連や医薬品関連のほか、 食料品関連の輸出に下押し圧力がかか
ったものの、 半導体など電子部品関連や精密機械関連のほか、 鉄鋼製品関連や鉱物資源関連の輸出
は旺盛に推移している。 原油関連を含む総輸出額も前年同月比＋51.5％となり、 前月 （同＋49.2％）
から伸びが加速した。前月比も＋5.1％と前月（同＋3.1％）から４ヵ月連続で拡大しており、単月
ベースの輸出額は過去最高を更新した。 一方、輸入額は前年同月比＋49.9％となり、前月（同＋
39.3％） から伸びが加速した。 前月比も＋12.4％と前月 （同▲0.3％） から２ヵ月ぶりの拡大に転じ
ており、 輸出と同様に持ち直しの動きをみせている。 輸出の堅調さを背景に、 設備機械関連や素材 ・

会议强调，继续实施适度宽松的货币政策，综合运⽤多种货币政策⼯具，保持流动性充
裕。做好⾦融 “ 五篇⼤⽂章 ” ，加快建设多层次现代化⾦融市场体系，提升⾦融服务实体经济

⽀付体系。坚定维护国家⾦融安全。
摘要：
2026 年 10 ⽉ 30 ⽇，中国⼈⺠银⾏党委书记、⾏⻓潘功胜主持召开党委会议，传达学习习近平总
书记在党的⼆⼗届五中全会上的重要讲话和全会精神，研究部署贯彻落实举措。中国⼈⺠银⾏
各党委委员参加会议。
会议认为，党的⼆⼗届四中全会以来，以习近平同志为核⼼的党中央坚持稳中求进⼯作总基
调，统筹国内国际两个⼤局，统筹发展和安全，有效应对各种外部冲击和内部困难挑战，扎实
推动经济⾼质量发展，纵深推进全⾯从严治党，开展树⽴和践⾏正确政绩观学习教育， “ ⼗四五 ”
圆满收官， “ ⼗五五 ” 开局良好，党和国家事业取得新进展新成效。中国⼈⺠银⾏坚决贯彻落实中

央决策部署，实施好适度宽松的货币政策，综合使⽤多种货币政策⼯具，保持流动性充裕，优
化调整⼀系列结构性货币政策⼯具，扎实有⼒做好⾦融 “ 五篇⼤⽂章 ” ，深化⾦融供给侧结构性改
⾰，防范化解重点领域⻛险，推动⾦融⾼⽔平开放，为经济平稳增⻓、⾼质量发展和⾦融市场
稳定运⾏营造了良好的货币⾦融条件。
会议指出，党的⼆⼗届五中全会回顾党 100 多年来的奋⽃历程，深刻指出中国共产党坚持为中国
⼈⺠谋幸福、为中华⺠族谋复兴，在各个历史时期都⾼度重视管党治党，勇于坚持真理、修正
错误，成为党和⼈⺠事业的坚强领导核⼼。党的⼗⼋⼤以来，以习近平同志为核⼼的党中央把
全⾯从严治党纳⼊ “ 四个全⾯ ” 战略布局，以巨⼤的政治勇⽓和强烈的责任担当，深⼊推进新时代
党的建设新的伟⼤⼯程，从根本上扭转了管党治党宽松软状况，开辟了百年⼤党⾃我⾰命新境
界，推动党和国家事业取得历史性成就、发⽣历史性变⾰。在新时代管党治党、兴党强党伟⼤
实践中，以习近平同志为核⼼的党中央提出⼀系列新理念新思想新战略，形成习近平党建思

想，丰富和发展了⻢克思主义建党学说，为深⼊推进新时代党的建设新的伟⼤⼯程提供了根本
遵循和⾏动指南。
会议认为，党的⼆⼗届五中全会审议通过的《关于持之以恒推进全⾯从严治党若⼲重⼤问题的
决定》，以习近平新时代中国特⾊社会主义思想为指导，深⼊贯彻习近平党建思想，系统总结新
时代全⾯从严治党宝贵经验，深⼊分析党的建设⾯临的新情况新问题，对持之以恒推进全⾯从
严治党作出系统部署，是持续推进新时代党的建设新的伟⼤⼯程的纲领性⽂件。
会议强调，中国⼈⺠银⾏将全⾯准确贯彻落实习近平总书记重要讲话和全会精神，坚持和加强
党中央对⾦融⼯作的集中统⼀领导，纵深推进全⾯从严治党，坚定⾛好中国特⾊⾦融发展之
路。
⼀是坚持以党的政治建设为统领。严格落实 “ 第⼀议题 ” 制度，把贯彻落实习近平总书记重要讲话
和重要指⽰批⽰精神放在⾸位，贯通落实党中央、国务院关于⾦融⼯作部署，健全学习贯彻闭
环落实⼯作机制。深⼊学习贯彻习近平党建思想，严格贯彻执⾏⺠主集中制。推动全体党员、
⼲部加强党性修养，把党的领导政治优势、制度优势转化为⾦融治理效能。

⼆是以更⾼标准更实举措贯彻落实党中央关于⾦融⼯作的重⼤部署。继续实施适度宽松的货币
政策，综合运⽤多种货币政策⼯具，保持流动性充裕。做好⾦融 “ 五篇⼤⽂章 ” ，加快建设多层次
现代化⾦融市场体系，提升⾦融服务实体经济适配性，更好⽀持经济转型升级和增⻓动能转
